&lt;project_context>
# 项目: schema_semantic_constrained_ie

## 运行环境
- 服务器名: westd-16980
- SSH 连接命令: ssh westd-16980  # 必须用此命令，~/.ssh/config 自动应用密钥和跳板机，禁止用裸 IP
- 项目代码根目录: /root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie


## 环境搭建
连接服务器后先调 `detect_image` 检测是否为预置镜像。如果匹配，跳过基础环境搭建，只装项目特有依赖。已预装的包不要重复安装。

## 输出风格
回复简洁直接。只输出执行结果和必要信息，不要解释过程、不要客套、不要给未被要求的建议。
&lt;/project_context>
## Task: R16 Supplementary Computations (4 Sub-tasks)

### Sub-task 1: WikiEvents κ̄ Calculation
Compute constraint tightness κ̄ for the WikiEvents event extraction schema.

κ̄ = mean over all event types r of (|σ(r)| / |A|²), where:
- σ(r) = set of valid (argument_role, entity_type) pairs for event type r
- |A| = total number of distinct entity types in the schema
- For each event type, count how many valid (role, type) combinations exist vs. total possible

SSH read-only to westd-21461, find the WikiEvents schema definition in `/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/`. Look for constraint tables, ontology files, or schema definition files that define:
- All event types (e.g., Contact.Meet, Life.Die, etc.)
- For each event type, valid argument roles and their allowed entity types
- The full entity type inventory

Calculate κ̄ and compare with:
- SciERC κ̄ = ~0.38 (tight constraints, high DR reduction)
- DocRED κ̄ = ~0.82 (loose constraints, lower DR reduction)

### Sub-task 2: 70B CF Analysis
From existing 70B inference results on westd-16980 or westd-21461, analyze the CF=7.01% breakdown:
- Find the 70B SciERC constrained output (exp_ids: 70b_scierc_constrained)
- Extract which relation types contribute most CF violations
- Compare CF distribution with 8B models' CF distribution
- Determine if this is INT4 quantization artifact or model scale effect

Look for output files in `/root/autodl-tmp/schema_outputs/` or the project's output directories.

### Sub-task 3: Citation Verification
Use WebSearch to verify these two bibliography entries are real:

1. **regino2025kgcurator**: "Can LLMs be Knowledge Graph Curators for Validating Triple Insertions?" by André Gomes Regino and Julio Cesar dos Reis
   - Previously verified at: https://aclanthology.org/2025.genaik-1.10/
   - Confirm it exists, verify venue (GenAIK Workshop, COLING 2025)

2. **formattax2026**: "The Format Tax" by Ivan Yee Lee, Loris D'Antoni, Taylor Berg-Kirkpatrick
   - Previously verified at: https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03616
   - Confirm it exists, verify author names

Report: REAL or HALLUCINATION for each, with evidence URL.

### Sub-task 4: ±0.6pp R-F1 Range Confirmation
The abstract claims "±0.6 pp" R-F1 impact. Verify this is accurate across ALL datasets, not just SciERC.

Find constrained vs unconstrained R-F1 deltas for:
- SciERC: Qwen FT and Llama FT (constrained S1 vs unconstrained)
- DocRED: Qwen FT and Llama FT (constrained S1 vs unconstrained)
- WikiEvents: Qwen FT and Llama FT (constrained vs unconstrained) — this uses Arg-F1 not R-F1

Check if ALL deltas fall within ±0.6pp. If not, identify which dataset/model exceeds this range.

Look in the registry for experiments with constrained vs unconstrained metrics, or in output files on the servers.

### Output
Write all results to local file: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_6/projects/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/artifacts/r16_verification/task_c_supplementary.json`

```text
I found the paper you're looking for! Here's the information about the research:

"Can LLMs be Knowledge Graph Curators for Validating Triple Insertions?" is a paper by André Gomes Regino and Julio Cesar dos Reis presented at the Workshop on Generative AI and Knowledge Graphs (GenAIK), pages 87–99, Abu Dhabi, UAE in 2025.

The paper investigates the feasibility of using LLMs to validate RDF triples, focusing on four distinct and complementary validation tasks: class and property alignment, URI standardization, semantic consistency, and syntax validation.

The paper is available at the ACL Anthology and addresses the complexity and scalability challenges in manual validation processes by exploring Large Language Models (LLMs) as potential automated validators.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

```text
Links: [{"title":"[2604.03616v1] The Format Tax","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03616v1"},{"title":"[2604.03616] The Format Tax","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.03616"},{"title":"The Format Tax","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2604.03616"},{"title":"The Format Tax | Trending arXiv papers – Ribbit Ribbit","url":"https://ribbitribbit.co/paper/arxiv.2604.03616-The-Format-Tax"},{"title":"dblp: Loris D'Antoni","url":"https://dblp.org/pid/85/770.html"},{"title":"The Format Tax","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2604.03616"},{"title":"Loris D'Antoni - Amazon Web Services (AWS)","url":"https://www.linkedin.com/in/loris-d-antoni-5a4b5a17/"}]

I found the paper! Here's what I found about "The Format Tax":

The paper was submitted on April 4, 2026 and authored by Ivan Yee Lee, Loris D'Antoni, and Taylor Berg-Kirkpatrick.

**Main Findings:**

The paper argues that asking a large language model to respond in JSON should be a formatting choice, not a capability tax. However, the researchers found that structured output requirements -- JSON, XML, LaTeX, Markdown -- substantially degrade reasoning and writing performance across open-weight models.

**Key Insights:**

While the research response has focused on constrained decoding, sampling bias accounts for only a fraction of the degradation. The dominant cost enters at the prompt: format-requesting instructions alone cause most of the accuracy loss, before any decoder constraint is applied.

**Proposed Solution:**

The paper points to a simple principle: decouple reasoning from formatting. Whether by generating freeform first and reformatting in a second pass, or by enabling extended thinking within a single generation, separating the two concerns substantially recovers lost accuracy. Across six open-weight models, four API models, four formats, and tasks spanning math, science, logic, and writing, decoupling recovers most lost accuracy.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.
```

_source line 51 · 2026-05-21T03:18:02.173Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
I have the ontology constraints file data. Now I need to:

1. Compute κ̄ for WikiEvents - I have the ontology_constraints_wikievents.json with meta info: 56 event types, 27 entity types, 245 role-entity constraints, 80 roles. I need to compute it properly using the formula.

Let me compute κ̄ on the server using Python. The formula is:
κ̄ = mean over all event types r of (|σ(r)| / |A|²)
where σ(r) = set of valid (argument_role, entity_type) pairs for event type r


|A| = 27 entity types, so |A|² = 729. For each event type, I'll count the valid role-entity pairs and divide by 729, then average across all 56 event types.

2. For the 70B models, I found unified_eval files with constrained and unconstrained versions for DocRED, but I'm not seeing a 70B SciERC variant even though the task references "exp_ids: 70b_scierc_constrained" - I need to check if those files exist or if they need to be generated.

3. Citations are confirmed as real.

4. Now I need to pull the R-F1 metrics from the d066 files and unified_eval outputs to compare constrained versus unconstrained performance, then compute κ̄ using Python and review the 70B analysis results along with the fine-tuning evaluation deltas.
```

```json
{
  "server": "westd-21461",
  "command": "python3 -c \"\nimport json\nwith open('/root/schema_semantic_constrained_ie/data/wikievents/ontology_constraints_wikievents.json') as f:\n    data = json.load(f)\nA = len(data['entity_types'])\nconstraints = data['event_type_constraints']\nratios = []\nfor etype, info in constraints.items():\n    sigma = sum(len(types) for types in info['role_entity_constraints'].values())\n    ratios.append(sigma / (A * A))\nkappa_bar = sum(ratios) / len(ratios)\nprint(f'entity_types={A}, event_types={len(constraints)}, A^2={A*A}')\nprint(f'kappa_bar={kappa_bar:.4f}')\nprint(f'min_ratio={min(ratios):.4f}, max_ratio={max(ratios):.4f}')\nsigmas = [sum(len(types) for types in info['role_entity_constraints'].values()) for info in constraints.values()]\nprint(f'mean_sigma={sum(sigmas)/len(sigmas):.1f}, min_sigma={min(sigmas)}, max_sigma={max(sigmas)}')\n\"\n"
}
```
